# The Author’s Report,
## How he found this path to Christ and walked upon it himself.
### An extract from his twelfth Epistle <note>original: *Send-Brief*, a formal letter intended for circulation among spiritual followers</note>.

May the open fountain-spring in the heart of Jesus Christ <note>original: *JEsu Christi*</note> be our refreshment, and lead us into Himself, so that we may live in His power, rejoice in Him, love, know, and enter into one will.

<margin>The author, as a child,</margin>
I shall not hide from you the simple path of a child which I walk in Christ. For I can write of myself in no other way than as of a child who knows and understands nothing, and has never learned anything, except only this: what the Lord wishes to know within me, according to the measure in which He reveals Himself in me.

<margin>relates his guidance,</margin>
For I never desired to know anything of the Divine Mystery <term>Mystery</term> <note>referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, or the hidden spiritual secrets of God and the universe</note>, much less understood how I should seek it,

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>This page introduces a personal testimony from the author, describing his spiritual journey as one of childlike simplicity and divine revelation rather than academic learning. He emphasizes that his knowledge of the "Divine Mystery" comes solely through God's grace and presence within him.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, spiritual journey, divine revelation, inner light, childlike faith, mysticism, epistle</keywords>

...could find; I knew nothing of it either, as is the way of the laity in their simplicity: I sought only the heart of **Jesus Christ** <note>original: *JEsu Christi*</note>, to hide myself therein from the grim wrath of **God** and the attacks of the devil, and I prayed to **God** earnestly for His Holy Spirit and grace, that **He** would bless and lead me in **Himself**, and take from me whatever turned me away from **Him**, and to surrender myself entirely to **Him**; so that I might live not according to my own will, but His, and that **He** alone might lead me, and I might be His child in His Son **Jesus Christ**.

<margin>

he was

through

</margin>
In such very earnest seeking and desiring of mine (in which I suffered violent obstacles <note>original: *Anstöße*, referring to spiritual or psychological struggles and opposition</note>, yet I would rather have risked my life than given up or desisted from it), the gate was opened to me, so that in one quarter of an hour I saw and knew more than if I had been for many years at the high schools <note>original: *hohen Schulen*, referring to universities</note>, at which I wondered so greatly that I knew not what was happening to me, and thereupon turned my heart to the praise of **God**.

</margin>

In Böhme's philosophy, this is the absolute 'non-ground' or nothingness that precedes all existence and even the self-revelation of God.</note>: likewise, the knowledge of the birth of the Holy **Trinity** <note>original: *Dreyfaltigkeit*</note> / the origin and the primary state of this world / and of all creatures / through the Divine Wisdom: I recognized / and saw in myself all three worlds / namely (1) the Divine / Angelic or Paradisiacal; and then (2) the dark world / as the primary state of nature leading to fire: and then (3) this external / visible world / as a creature and offspring / or as a spoken-forth being <note>original: *ausgesprochen Wesen*. This refers to the idea that the physical world is the 'expressed' or 'spoken' manifestation of the inner spiritual word.</note> out of both inner spiritual worlds. I saw and recognized the whole Essence in Evil and Good / how one originated from the other / and how the Mother of the birthing-one was <note>original: *Mutter der Gebärerin*; likely referring to the source of all generative nature</note> / so that I did not only wonder greatly / but also rejoiced.

write.

In the interior, I saw it well / as if in a great depth / for I saw through it

# Preface.
)( 4
<meta>catchword: I</meta>

...as if into a chaos <note>original: *Chaos*. In Böhme's time, this did not just mean "disorder," but the primordial "gap" or state of unformed potential where all things exist but are not yet distinct.</note>, where everything lies within / but its unfolding <term>unfolding</term> <note>original: *Auswickelung*. This refers to the process of latent spiritual truths becoming explicit and understandable.</note> was impossible for me.

<margin>Twelve years passed before he could bring it into the external world.</margin>
But it opened itself from time to time within me / as in a growing plant; although I carried it with me for twelve years / and was pregnant with it within / and felt a violent urge in me / before I could bring it into the external: Until it afterward overtook me like a sudden downpour <note>original: *Platz-Regen*. A vivid metaphor for a burst of inspiration that strikes suddenly and forcefully.</note> / whatever it hits, it hits; so it went with me too / whatever I could grasp to bring into the external / that I wrote down.

<margin>When the sun did not shine for him, he did not recognize his own work.</margin>
Although the sun <note>In this context, the "sun" represents the light of divine revelation or spiritual clarity.</note> shone for me for a considerable time thereafter / yet not always persistently: When it hid itself / then I too could hardly understand my own work / and this for the purpose / that man may recognize / that knowledge is not his own / but **God's** / that **God** knows in the soul of man / what and how He wills.

<margin>Wrote from his own book.</margin>
So I have now written / not from human teaching or science gained from book-learning / but from my own book <note>The "own book" refers to the internal revelation written upon the human soul, rather than external academic texts.</note> / which was opened within me / as the noble likeness of **God**; the book of the noble image (to be understood as the image of **God**) I was permitted to read / and in it have I...

<summary>Böhme describes a twelve-year period of internal gestation before his spiritual insights burst forth like a sudden downpour, emphasizing that his knowledge comes from divine illumination within his own soul rather than academic study.</summary>

# Preface.
)( 4

...I found my study / like a child in its mother’s house / who sees what the father does / and mimics him in child’s play; I need no other book for this.

<margin>...has only three leaves.</margin>
My book has only three leaves <note>In Böhme’s mystical language, "leaves" refers to the fundamental layers of revelation or reality, rather than literal paper pages.</note> / these are the three **Principles** <term>Principles</term> <note>original: *Principia*. These represent Böhme's core metaphysical structure: the Dark World (God's wrath/fire), the Light World (God's love), and the External/Visible World.</note> of eternity; therein I can find everything / that Moses and the prophets / as well as **Christ** and the **Apostles** have spoken. I can find the world’s foundation and all mysteries <note>original: *Heimlichkeit*. Refers to the hidden, interior secrets of divine creation.</note> within it: Yet it is not I / but the Spirit of the Lord who does it / according to the measure that He wills.

<margin>...has often prayed against worldly knowledge,</margin>
For I have pleaded with Him many hundred times / that if my knowledge might not serve His honor and the improvement of my brothers / He would take it from me / and keep me only in His love: But I found / that with my pleading I only ignited the fire <note>Fire is a central theme for Böhme, representing the driving force of the soul and the transformative power of God’s presence.</note> in me more fiercely / and in such ignition and recognition <term>recognition</term> <note>original: *Erkäntniß*. This term implies an experiential, spiritual "realization" rather than just intellectual learning.</note> I have produced my writings.

<margin>...and did not think to become known by people.</margin>
I did not intend by this to become known among such people / as I see now / I always thought / I was writing for myself; although the Spirit of God in the

# Preface.

...hiddenness in my spirit showed this sufficiently / for what end it might be / yet outer reason <term>reason</term> <note>original: *Vernunft*. In Böhme's context, this refers to human logic and worldly intellectual effort, which he often contrasts with divine revelation.</note> was always the opposite <note>original: *Contrarium*. A Latin term meaning the polar opposite or adversary.</note>: Except only at times / when the **Morning Star** <note>original: *Morgenstern*. A symbol for the dawning of divine light or Christ's presence within the human soul.</note> rose / then was reason also ignited / and danced along / as if it had grasped it / but it is far from it.

<margin>God dwells in the likeness.</margin>
**God** dwells in the noble likeness <note>original: *Bildniß*. This refers to the "image of God" (imago Dei) within a person, which Böhme believes is the only place where God can truly reside in a human being.</note> / and not in the spirit of the stars and elements; He possesses nothing / but only Himself in His own kind. And although He does possess Something / as He then possesses Everything / yet nothing grasps Him / except only that / which has sprung and come from Him / such as the soul in the likeness of **God**.

<margin>It is a student’s writing,</margin>
Therefore all my writing is like that of a student / who goes to school; **God** has led my soul into a wondrous school / and I can in truth claim nothing for myself / as if my "I-hood" <term>I-hood</term> <note>original: *Ichheit*. A term Böhme uses to describe the self-centered ego or the "self" that acts independently of God’s will.</note> were anything or understood anything.

<margin>the work is God’s.</margin>
No one should think more highly of me / than he sees here: For the work in my labor is not mine / I have it only according to the measure / as it is granted to me by the **Lord**; I am only His tool / with which He does / what He wills. I report this...

<meta>catchword: there-</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that his insights come from God dwelling within the soul's "likeness," rather than from human reason or the physical world. He humbles himself as a mere student and a tool of the divine, cautioning readers not to credit his own intellect for the work.</summary>
<keywords>reason vs. revelation, Morning Star, Image of God, I-hood, divine tool, soul, inner school</keywords>

# Preface. 9

...therefore for your information / so that no one should seek another person in me / whom I am not / such as a man of art and high reason <term>reason</term> <note>original: *Vernunft*. In Böhme’s thought, this is the human capacity for logic and sensory learning, which he views as limited compared to divine revelation.</note>; rather I live in weakness and childhood / in the simplicity of Christ / in my given child-work <note>original: *Kinder-Werke*. Böhme views his writing not as a heavy academic burden, but as a form of spiritual "play" or simple labor granted to him by God.</note> / therein I have my play / and it is my pastime / Therein I have my joy / as if in a pleasure-garden / where many noble flowers stand within; with these I will delight myself for a while / until I shall again attain the Paradise-flowers in the new man <note>original: *im neuen Menschen*. A reference to the "reborn" spiritual self that regains the state of perfection lost by Adam.</note>.

One's own reason / which without **God's** Spirit is taught merely by the letter <note>original: *Buchstaben*. This refers to a literal or academic understanding of scripture and laws, devoid of living spiritual insight.</note> / blames and despises everything / that does not align straight as a string according to the law of the high schools <note>original: *hohen Schulen*. A term for universities and the academic establishment of the 17th century.</note>. But I am not at all surprised / for she <note>In German, "Reason" (*Vernunft*) is a feminine noun; Böhme personifies it here.</note> is from the outside / and **God's** Spirit is from within; She is good and evil / she passes away like a wind / and lets herself be weighed and driven / she pays heed to human judgment / and whatever the high prestige of this world judges / according to that she also judges; she does not recognize the **Lord’s** mind / for He is not in her; Her understanding is from the stars <term>stars</term> <note>original: *Gestirne*. This refers to the astral or "sidereal" spirit—the natural influence of the cosmos on the human mind, which Böhme considers inferior to the divine Light.</note> / and is only a mirror against the **Divine Wisdom**.

<meta>This page continues from page 9, where Böhme explains that his writing comes from spiritual joy rather than academic study. ## Preface. 10

<margin>
Reason judges the other from the outside; the Spirit of **God** from the inside.
</margin>
Who can judge divine matters if the Spirit of the Lord is not within them? The Spirit of the Lord alone judges and examines all things: For to Him alone all things are known and manifest. Human reason, however, judges from the outside, and one person's reason always judges according to another’s: the small according to the great, the layman according to the <term>doctor</term> <note>original: *Doctor*. In Böhme’s time, this specifically referred to a highly educated academic or a Doctor of Divinity.</note>. Thus, no one grasps the truth or the Lord’s mind except for the Spirit of God, which judges within the human being and is no respecter of persons <note>a biblical phrase meaning God does not show favoritism based on social status or rank</note>: to Him, the layman is the same as the doctor.

<margin>
Conflict over the letter has grown out of the diverse gifts of the children of God.
</margin>
But because the children of **God** have such a variety of gifts for writing, speaking, and judging, and do not all follow the same style, one’s own reason thereafter sucks out its own profit and creates a **Babel** <note>Böhme uses "Babel" to represent the confusion, sectarianism, and prideful intellectualism of the organized church of his day.</note>. From this, so many different opinions have arisen, as people have devised their own views and "ways to God" out of those holy writings—insisting on which paths one ought to walk. Thus, such a quarrel has arisen that, at present, man looks only upon the dispute, seeing who can overcome the other through an exchange of letters <note>original: *Buchstaben-Wechseln*. This refers to pedantic arguments over the literal text of scripture or academic definitions rather than spiritual experience.</note>. That is all **Babel**, a mother of spiritual harlotry <note>a common 17th-century metaphor for the corruption of the church through human pride and false doctrines</note>, where reason does not enter through the door of Christ, through Christ’s Spirit into the

<meta>catchword: Ge- (the beginning of the word "Geheimnis" [mystery] or "Geist" [spirit] on the following page)</meta>

<summary>Böhme argues that divine truth can only be understood through the inner Spirit of God rather than external academic learning. He criticizes institutional religious conflict (Babel) as a result of human logic trying to categorize spiritual gifts into rigid dogmas.</summary>
<keywords>reason vs. spirit, Babel, sectarianism, spiritual harlotry, inner judgment, Jacob Böhme, religious conflict</keywords>

### Preface. II

[...does not] enter through <term>yieldedness</term> <note>original: *Gelassenheit*. A central concept for Böhme, referring to a state of spiritual surrender or letting go of the self-will to allow God to work within.</note>; rather, it forces itself in by its own power and <term>pride</term> <note>original: *Hoffart*. This refers to a specific type of spiritual arrogance or haughtiness.</note> into another person, and always wants to be the fairest child in the house, demanding to be honored and worshipped.

<margin>As each person’s soulful spirit is constellated, so is their knowledge.</margin>
The children of **God** have various gifts, according to the rule of the Apostle: **God** gives to each to speak as He wills. Human gifts all occur according to the unsearchable will of **God**, and all spring from one root, which is the mother of the <term>Three Principles</term> <note>In Böhme’s cosmology, the "Three Principles" represent the three "worlds" or dimensions of reality: the dark fire-world (the Father’s power), the light-world (the Son’s love), and the outer visible world.</note>. As each person's soulful spirit is <term>constellated</term> <note>Böhme uses the language of astrology—"constellated"—to describe a person's inner spiritual makeup or character, shaped by eternal forces.</note> in the eternal mother, so also is their revelation and knowledge.

<margin>God opens our spirit with His Spirit.</margin>
For **God** brings no new or foreign spirit into us; instead, He opens our own spirit with His Spirit, revealing the hidden things of the wisdom of **God** which lie within every human being, according to the measure and manner of their inner, hidden constellation. For Christ said: *My Father works, and I work also.* [John 5:17]. Thus the Father works in the essence of the soulful quality, and the Son works in the essence of the <term>Image of God</term> <note>original: *Ebenbildes GOttes*. This refers to the original divine likeness in humanity that Böhme believes is being restored through Christ.</note>, as in the divine likeness.

<meta>catchword: Of</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that spiritual knowledge is not forced through intellectual pride but is a divine awakening of the wisdom already latent within the human soul, tailored to each individual's unique spiritual makeup.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the preface where Böhme explained that God opens the human spirit from within. Here, he addresses the diversity of spiritual gifts and the danger of using human reason to judge others' revelations, emphasizing that all true prophecy flows from the same divine source.</meta>

### Preface.

<margin>
The 
prophets 
did not 
employ 
the same forms.
</margin>
From the beginning of the world, all prophets have prophesied of **Christ** <note>original: "Christo" (Latin dative). Böhme frequently uses Latin inflections for the name of Christ.</note>; one in this way, the other in another. They have not all conducted their speech in one and the same form; rather, each one as the **Spirit of God** has opened to him in his soulful, eternal <term>constellation</term> <note>As noted on the previous page, Böhme uses "constellation" to mean the unique spiritual and psychological makeup of an individual, shaped by eternal forces.</note>; but they have all spoken from **One Center** <note>original: "Einem Centro". In Böhme’s theology, the "Center" is the divine heart or the point of origin from which all life and light flow.</note>. Thus it still happens today: The children of **God** all speak from the opening of the **Spirit of Christ**, which is **God's**, each according to his own understanding. I would therefore have you kindly reminded not to take offense at the <term>chatter of reason</term> <note>original: "Vernunft-Geschwätze". This refers to human intellectualism that attempts to analyze divine things without spiritual inspiration.</note> and proud judgments, and thereby to despise anyone's gifts: for he who does such a thing, despises the **Spirit of God**.

<margin>
Condemnation
is a 
false 
chatter.
</margin>
I judge no one, and condemnation is a false chatter; the **Spirit of God** Himself judges all things; if the same is in us, why then do we ask so long after the chatter? Rather, I rejoice much more in the gifts of my brothers. But if it be that they have had another gift to express than I, should I judge them for that?

<margin>
We 
are more 
foolish.
</margin>
Does one herb, flower, or tree say to another: "You are sour and dark, I do not want to stand beside you!" Have they not all...

<summary>Böhme argues that spiritual diversity is a divine necessity, asserting that while prophets and believers may speak in different "forms" based on their inner nature, they all draw from the same divine Center. He warns against using human reason to judge or condemn the unique spiritual gifts of others, using an analogy of plants in nature to illustrate harmony in diversity.</summary>

<keywords>prophets, Spirit of Christ, spiritual gifts, Center, reason, judgment, tolerance, constellation, nature analogy</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument for spiritual unity, comparing the diversity of human souls to flowers growing from the same earth. Böhme warns against pride and sectarian judgment (Babel), pointing to humility as the true mark of a child of God.</meta>

### Preface.

<margin>
...like the flowers.
</margin>
...all [have] One Mother, from which they grow? So also all souls [come] from One, and all people from One. Why do we boast of being children of **God**, when we are yet more senseless than the flowers and the herbs in the field? Is it not also the case with us, that **God** reveals His wisdom in us, just as He reveals the <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur". In Böhme's philosophy, the "tincture" is the spiritual essence or life-force that transforms and beautifies matter from within.</note> of the hiddenness in the earth, through the earth with beautiful plants? So it is also in us humans. We should much rather rejoice in this, and love one another heartily, that **God** reveals His wisdom in us in such manifold ways. But he who judges and condemns in a godless way, running only in <term>pride</term> <note>original: "Hofart". An archaic German term for haughty arrogance or vanity.</note> to make a show of himself—he is the driver of <term>Babel</term> <note>Böhme uses "Babel" to signify the confused state of human-made systems and intellectual strife that lack the true Spirit.</note>, a turning wheel that only stirs up <note>original: "aufbläset", literally "blows up" or "inflates".</note> strife.

<margin>
The test of the children of God is humility.
</margin>
The right <term>test</term> <note>original: "Proba". Latin for proof, trial, or examination.</note> of the children of **God** is this, which one may safely follow: namely, a humble heart that does not seek or honor itself, but always seeks its brother in love; that does not seek self-interest and honor, but righteousness and the fear of **God**. The right and <term>simple</term> <note>original: "schlechte". In the 17th century, "schlecht" meant plain, straight, or simple, rather than "bad" or "poor" as it does in modern German.</note> way to come to **God** is this, as far as it is recognizable to me: Namely, that

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>Böhme argues that all souls share a single divine origin, comparing human diversity to different flowers growing from the same soil. He identifies humility and love as the true evidence of spiritual life, contrasting them with the prideful "driver of Babel" who stirs up conflict.</summary>
<keywords>unity of souls, divine wisdom, tincture, humility vs. pride, Babel, the simple way to God, spiritual test</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the general nature of the soul discussed on page 13 to the specific practice of repentance. Böhme emphasizes that the seeker must abandon intellectual doubt and self-will, returning to a state of childlike trust in the "Mother"—a term he uses for the divine source or the wisdom of God.</meta>

### ->Preface.<-

...man should depart from his committed sins / and make an earnest resolution / never to enter into them again / and should not doubt in his departure <note>This "departure" refers to the act of turning away from a sinful life and moving toward God.</note>.

<margin>Should surrender to God's will.</margin>
And although reason certainly doubts / at which the sinner is terrified / and is horrified before **God's** wrath / let the will simply and truly sink itself into the mercy of **God** / into **Christ's** suffering and death / and surrender itself through **Christ** into **God** / as a child in its mother’s lap / who wants nothing for itself / but only what the mother wants; it only cries out to the mother / and always hopes for the best from the mother / it longs only for the mother’s breasts: So must our desire be simply directed back into our first Mother / from whom we, with Adam, have gone out into <term>selfhood</term> <note>original: "ein Eignes". In Böhme’s thought, this is the state of being "own-willed" or self-centered, which caused the fall of humanity.</note>.

<margin>Become like a child.</margin>
Therefore **Christ** says: Unless you turn / and become like children / you shall not see the kingdom of **God**. Matt. 18:3. Also <note>original: "Item", a Latin term meaning "likewise" or "furthermore," commonly used in early modern German scholarly writing.</note>: You must be born again / John 3:7. That is / we must go out of all reason and back into <term>resignation</term> <note>original: "Gelassenheit". This is a central term in German mysticism, describing a state of perfect yieldedness, letting go of the ego, and quiet surrender to the divine will.</note> / into our Mother’s lap / and let go of all disputing / also our reason...

<summary>Böhme describes true repentance as a return to a childlike state of total surrender, or Gelassenheit, where the believer moves beyond intellectual doubt to rest in God's mercy. He uses the maternal metaphor of a child in its mother's lap to illustrate the soul's total dependence on its divine source.</summary>
<keywords>repentance, Gelassenheit, resignation, spiritual rebirth, self-will, reason, childlikeness, divine mercy</keywords>

### ->Preface.<-

<note>The text begins with the second half of the word "Vernunft" (Reason).</note> reason must be made entirely as if dead / so that the Mother’s Spirit <note>Böhme often refers to Divine Wisdom (Sophia) as the "Mother" or "Mother Spirit" who births the divine life in the soul.</note> may take shape in us / and breathe the Divine Life into us / so that we find ourselves in the Mother’s Spirit in the cradle / if we wish to be taught and driven by **God**.

We must surrender ourselves entirely to Him / so that **God’s** Spirit may be the willing / doing / and accomplishing in us; so that we know Him / and not ourselves: so that He may be our knowledge.

<margin>enter into Christ’s suffering.</margin>
We should by no means say what we want to know / but only simply enter into the incarnation and birth of **Jesus Christ** / into his suffering and death / and always gladly wish to walk in his footsteps / and follow Him / and remember / that we are on the pilgrim’s path / where we must travel through a dangerous way into our fatherland / out of which Adam led us / to enter again in **Christ** upon the narrow path.

<margin>In this narrow way lies the pearl</margin>
On this single way lies the pearl / the Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum". This Latin term refers to the "Great Mystery" or the divine "chaos" from which all things are created—a central concept in Böhme’s cosmology.</note>. All studying / seeking and searching outside of this way is dead / and does not obtain the Virginly Crown <note>A symbol of the soul’s spiritual union with Divine Wisdom.</note> / but only thorns and points / which prick the children of **God**.

<summary>Böhme argues that intellectual reasoning must be abandoned in favor of a childlike surrender to the "Mother Spirit" and Christ's suffering. He describes the spiritual life as a dangerous pilgrimage back to a lost fatherland, where the "Great Mystery" can only be found through humility rather than academic study.</summary>

### ->Preface.<-

<margin>Points him to his own way.</margin>
Therefore, to you who desire my confession <note>In this context, a "confession" refers to a formal statement of faith or a spiritual testimony.</note>, I know of no better advice to share than to show you the way that I myself walk, upon which the door was opened to me, so that I have been taught without previously learning. For all art and knowledge come from **God**, who finds all things.

<margin>Without strife.</margin>
I have no quarrel with the children of **God** regarding their unequal gifts; I can unite them all within me; I go with them only to the center <note>original: "Centrum"</note>, and so I have the proof <term>Probam: a Latin term for a test, trial, or standard of proof</term> of all things. If you wish to follow me now, you will experience it, and perhaps afterward understand better what I have written.

<margin>A Christian strives with no one.</margin>
A true Christian has no conflict with anyone, for through yieldedness <term>Gelassenheit: a central concept in German mysticism referring to a state of selfless surrender and letting go of one's own will to God</term> in **Christ**, he dies to all strife. He no longer worries about the way to **God**, but rather surrenders himself into the Mother <note>Refers again to Divine Wisdom or the Holy Spirit as the nurturing source of the soul.</note>—that is, into **Christ’s** Spirit—and whatever that Spirit does with him is all the same to him. Whether it be fortune or misfortune in this world, life or death, it is all equal to him. No misfortune touches the new man, but only the old man of this world; the world may do with him as it wishes. That old man belongs to the world, but the new man belongs to **God**.

<summary>Böhme explains that true spiritual knowledge comes from God rather than study, and that a believer finds peace through "Gelassenheit" (yieldedness), which allows them to remain untouched by the world's troubles.</summary>
<keywords>confession, divine knowledge, Gelassenheit, spiritual surrender, the new man, Christ's Spirit</keywords>

I do not set any purpose for myself beforehand as to what I will write or speak; rather, I surrender myself to **God’s** knowing. He may know within me whatever He wills. # Preface.

<margin>One can serve **God** everywhere.</margin>
**God** is in heaven, and heaven is in man; but if man wishes to be in heaven, then heaven must be made manifest within man. This must happen through earnest repentance and heartfelt yieldedness <note>original: "Einergeben." This is closely related to the mystical concept of *Gelassenheit*, or the total surrender of one's own will to God.</note>; they can certainly do that at home and in their own places. <margin>Judgment concerning Stiefel’s followers.</margin> <note>Referring to the adherents of Isaiah Stiefel (1561–1627), a contemporary mystic whose views on the perfection of the soul led to significant controversy and accusations of arrogance from other reformers like Böhme.</note>

For I have, in a completely Christian and brotherly way, requested and instructed one among them concerning a published booklet wherein I found something difficult against **God** and the foundation of truth; I hoped he would become seeing. But he answered quite proudly and contemptuously, and moreover mockingly, and gave such an answer in which no Spirit of **God** can be sensed.

# Preface.

...is. Their confession <note>original: "Confession." Here meaning their public profession of faith or their specific religious sect.</note> is more of an opinion than a true earnestness; for they are not that which they boast themselves to be. There may well be pious hearts among them, but many of them are so in name only and desire to have the appearance of piety; as I have personally experienced from one of the most prominent among them.

<margin>Reviling is Babel.</margin>
Would to **God** there were such earnestness in them as they pretend; I would then also praise it. But reviling and despising is merely Babel <note>In Böhme’s terminology, "Babel" refers to the confused, hypocritical state of the established churches and the world at large, characterized by pride and conflict rather than divine love.</note>, of which the world is full.

<margin>Concerning Hans Weyrauch.</margin>
As for Hans Weyrauch <note>A contemporary of Böhme and an associate of Isaiah Stiefel; Weyrauch was known for his mystical writings which sometimes clashed with Böhme's broader philosophical "Light of Nature."</note>, as far as I can see in the writing sent to me, he may be a person who dwells in **God’s** love, provided the path of his heart remains so. However, as for his blaming others regarding the knowledge of the light of nature <term>Light of Nature: *Licht der Natur*, a central concept for Böhme referring to the intuitive understanding of the cosmos and the divine through the physical world, seen as a complement to biblical revelation.</term>, he perhaps lacks knowledge in that area, and his gifts do not extend so far; this should not be held against him, because it is not his gift. For the time being, you should regard him as a pious brother. For **God** carries out His gifts not only in simplicity <term>Simplicity: *Einfalt*, the state of humble, unlearned, and pure-hearted faith, often contrasted with intellectual pride.</term>, but also in many things in the heights; for He is high and does what He will with all His works.

# Preface.

)( )( 3

Regarding the divinity <term>Divinity: *Gottheit*, the eternal, unmanifested essence of God.</term>, He is not a creature; but regarding the Heavenly Essence <term>Heavenly Essence: *Himlische Wesenheit*, the spiritual substance or "spiritual corporeality" that makes up the divine realm.</term>, of which He says He came from heaven and was in heaven, *John 3:13*, He is creaturely with that essence within His humanity, and uncreaturely outside of His humanity.

Just as we humans live in the four elements and are ourselves the property of the four elements: in us they are formed <note>original: *bildlich*, meaning they take on a specific, recognizable shape or image within the human body.</note>, and outside of us they are unformed, and yet they remain one single thing; so it is also in Christ’s person.

<margin>The Angelic World is God’s bodily essence.</margin>
The entire Angelic World <note>original: *Englische Welt*. In Böhme's vocabulary, "Englisch" refers to angels (*Engel*), not the English nation or language.</note>, as the second principle <term>Second Principle: *das 2te Principium*, Böhme’s term for the world of light, love, and divine clarity, associated with the Son.</term>, is His bodily essence according to the Heavenly Essence. Within the person of His humanity, it is creaturely, and outside that person, it is uncreaturely: For He is the Father’s heart and word, and the heart is everywhere in the Father; thus, where His heart is, there also is heaven and the Divine Essence, surrounded by the fullness of wisdom.

<margin>Christ’s soul is from our nature.</margin>
Concerning His soul, which He commended into His Father’s hands and of which He said on the Mount of Olives that it was sorrowful even unto death, it is from our own soulful nature <note>original: *Eigenschaft*, meaning property or essential characteristic.</note>. For it was for the sake of the soul that God became man, so that He might bring it back into Himself, and [our...]

<margin>The 3rd Principle is also creaturely in him.</margin>
And the 3rd Principle <term>3rd Principle: *das 3te Principium*, Böhme’s term for the physical, material world, which he viewed as a temporal reflection of the eternal divine world.</term>, as the outer kingdom of this world, which God has born out of eternity through His wisdom, is also creaturely within Him. For the entire Divinity <term>Divinity: *Gottheit*, the eternal, unmanifested essence of God.</term> has revealed itself in the man Christ; just as God is everything in this spirit, so He is also everything in this man. We humans are all like this, as far as we are born again out of God; and if this point—which drives almost all the others—were rightly considered, there would likely be no need for much strife or condemnation. For the Spirit of God asks for no strife; He settles <note>original: *richtet*, which can mean to judge, settle, or align.</note> all things within Himself.

<margin>A bee seeks the best from the flower.</margin>
I do not hold them (the aforementioned authors) <note>original: *obige Autores*, referring to spiritual writers like Caspar Schwenkfeld or Valentin Weigel mentioned on the preceding pages.</note> in contempt, nor the one who reads them. After all, a bee gathers honey from many flowers, even if one flower might be better than another; why should the bee care about that? It takes what serves it. Should it therefore thrust its sting into the flowers because it does not like the nectar, as the contemptuous person does? People fight over the husks <note>original: *Hülsen*, a metaphor for external dogmas or the "letter" of the law which lacks the living spiritual "kernel."</note>,

What does knowledge <term>knowledge: *Wissenschaft*, referring here to intellectual learning or theoretical theology rather than modern science.</term> profit me if I do not live within it? The cloak of Christ’s suffering and atonement <term>atonement: *Genugtuhung*, the theological concept of Christ's sacrifice satisfying the debt of sin.</term>, which people now wrap around themselves, will become a snare and hellish fire for many; for they only wish to tickle themselves with Christ’s atonement while keeping the rogue <note>original: *Schalk*, an old term Böhme uses to describe the unregenerate, sinful ego or "the old Adam."</note> within.

<margin>and a new man</margin>

Do not merely quarrel over knowledge, but become a new person who lives in righteousness and holiness in God. One must drive out the rogue and put on Christ; then we are in Christ, and buried with Christ in His death, and rise with Christ, and live eternally in Him. Why then should I quarrel for long over what I myself am?

# Preface.

[Having placed the] Spirit before your eyes: I did not wish to hide [this] from you, and I mean it faithfully.

To the clever and the satisfied, the high-minded and those who are self-sufficient in their knowledge, who can walk on their own and are already rich—to those I have written nothing. Instead, I write to the children and the innocent <term>innocent: *Unmündige*, literally "minors" or those without a legal voice; in Böhme’s theology, this refers to those with a humble, child-like spirit receptive to God.</term>, who suck at their mother’s breasts and are just learning to walk.

Whoever can understand it, let them understand it; but whoever cannot, let them leave it unmolested and uncriticized, for I have written nothing for them; I have written for myself.

Yet if my brother should be thirsty and ask me for water <note>Water here serves as a metaphor for spiritual truth or divine revelation flowing through the author.</note>, I will give him to drink, and he will experience what I have given him, if the Lord permits him to drink. And I commend myself to the reader’s favor, and all of us into the gentle love of Jesus Christ. Dated at Görlitz on the day of the Assumption of Mary <note>original: *Mariä Himmelfahrt*, a Christian feast day celebrating Mary being taken into heaven, observed on August 15th.</note>, in August 1622.

# Preface.

) ( ( 5

### From the Book of the Threefold Life of Man / Chapter 7, Section 10.

## How we must seek again that which was lost, etc.

**10.** O you poor, strayed soul in <term>Babylon: *Babel*, which in Böhme’s writing symbolizes the confusion of worldly languages, conflicting religious dogmas, and the pride of human intellect.</term>! What are you doing? O, desist from all opinions, whatever they may be called in this world; it is all merely a conflict of reason <note>Böhme uses "reason" (*Vernunft*) to describe the limited human intellect that tries to understand divine things without spiritual rebirth.</note>.

<margin>Christ is found neither in conflict nor in clever reason.</margin>
**11.** One finds the new rebirth and the **noble stone** <term>noble stone: *edler Stein*, an alchemical metaphor for the "Philosophers' Stone," which Böhme uses to represent the transformative power of Christ within the soul.</term> neither in conflict nor in any clever reason. You must let go of everything in this world—no matter how brightly it may shine—and enter into yourself. Gather all your sins, in which you are imprisoned, together into one heap and cast them into the mercy of God; flee to God and ask Him for forgiveness and for the enlightenment of His Spirit.

<margin>There must only be earnestness; heaven must burst open, and hell must tremble.</margin>
**12.** Do not dispute for long; only be earnest! For heaven must burst open and hell must tremble—and it shall indeed happen. You must set all your senses, together with your reason and everything that comes in your way, into this [purpose], so that you will not let go of Him until He blesses you, just as **Jacob** <note>original: *Jacob*; a reference to Genesis 32:24-30, where Jacob wrestles with a divine being all night and refuses to let go until he receives a blessing. Böhme sees this as a model for the soul's struggle.</note> did all night long with 

<meta>catchword: God</meta>

<summary>This page contains an excerpt from Böhme’s "The Threefold Life of Man," urging the reader to abandon intellectual religious disputes in favor of a deep, personal "earnestness" to achieve spiritual rebirth. He uses the alchemical "noble stone" and the biblical story of Jacob's wrestling as metaphors for this intense internal transformation.</summary>

--- Page 34 ---
<meta>This page continues the excerpt from Jacob Böhme’s "The Threefold Life of Man," specifically following section 12 where the reader was exhorted to be earnest in their spiritual pursuit. # Preface.

God wrestled. <note>Böhme frequently invokes the image of Jacob wrestling the angel (Genesis 32) as a metaphor for the soul's intense internal struggle to overcome doubt and reach God.</note> Even if your conscience says a resounding "No"—that God does not want you—I nonetheless want Him; I will not let go of You until they carry me to the grave; my will be Your will; I want what You, Lord, want. And even if all the devils stood around you and said, "Stop, that is enough for once," you must say: "No, my mind and will shall not depart from God; they shall be in God eternally; His love is greater than all my sin." If you, devils and the world, have this mortal body in your prison, I have my Savior and **Re-generator** <term>Re-generator: *Wiedergebährer*, a term Böhme uses to describe Christ's role in the "New Birth," actively recreating the divine image within the human soul.</term> in my soul; He will give me a heavenly body that remains forever.

<margin>You will soon receive a helper; Many words are not necessary.</margin>
**13.** Just try it in this way, and you will experience wonders; you will soon receive one within you who will help you wrestle, fight, and pray; and even if you cannot find many words, it does not matter—even if you could only manage that single word of the **publican** <term>publican: *Zöllner*, the tax collector from the biblical parable in Luke 18:13, who, unlike the Pharisee, prayed simply and humbly: "God, be merciful to me, a sinner."</term>: "O God, be merciful to me, a sinner!" But when your will, with all your reason and senses, is set upon God, determined not to let go of Him even if body and soul should shatter; then you hold God fast, and you break
<meta>catchword: through (brichst durch)</meta>

<summary>This page describes the soul's defiant struggle against doubt and demonic interference, emphasizing that a steadfast will is more important than eloquent prayer. Böhme encourages the reader to persist in seeking God until they experience an internal spiritual transformation.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual wrestling, New Birth, conscience, Savior, publican's prayer, will vs. reason, persistence</keywords>

...you break through death, hell, and heaven, and enter into the temple of Jesus Christ, against all the defenses of the devils. <margin>God's wrath cannot hold you.</margin> God's wrath cannot hold you, however great and powerful it may be within you; and even if body and soul were burning in that wrath and stood in the midst of hell among all the devils, you would still tear yourself out and arrive in the temple of Christ. There you receive the little wreath of pearls, set with that noble and highly venerable stone, the **angular stone of the philosophers** <note>original: *Lapide Philosophorum angulari*. Böhme is using alchemical language here. The "Philosopher’s Stone" was the legendary substance said to turn lead to gold, but for Böhme, it represents Christ—the "cornerstone" that transforms the soul's base nature into divine gold.</note>.

14. But know this: the kingdom of heaven is sown within
<margin>The kingdom of heaven is still like a tiny mustard seed.</margin>
you in this way, and it is as small as a mustard seed <note>A reference to Jesus’s parable in Matthew 13:31–32, suggesting that spiritual life begins as a tiny, almost invisible internal change.</note>. You will certainly receive great joy because of this **angelic wreath** <term>angelic wreath: *Englischen Kranze*. In the German of this period, "englisch" can mean both "English" and "angelic." Given the context of the soul's reward, it refers to the crown or state of being like an angel.</term>; but take care: do not place it upon the **old Adam** <term>old Adam: *alten Adam*, the fallen, sinful human nature that remains until death.</term>, or it will happen to you just as it did to Adam.

->Hold fast what you have,<-
->To suffer want is an evil guest.<-

<margin>You must face the tree of temptation and the world's mockery.</margin>
grows, provided it remains standing in good soil; many a cold, harsh wind whistles over a little branch before it grows into a tree. It is unstable; you must stand before the tree of temptation, and also in the wilderness of the world's mockery. If you do not hold fast, you have nothing; if you uproot your little branch, then...

A mystical setting symbolizing the state of divine love and spiritual growth, often contrasted with the "thistles" of the fallen world.</term>, hidden from the **old Adam**. Böhme uses it as a metaphor for Christ, who transforms the "base metal" of the sinful soul into "divine gold."</note>; mark this well!

# I.
## OF TRUE REPENTANCE,
<note>original Latin: *DE POENITENTIA VERA*</note>
->Book 1.<-
->Or:<-

# Of True Repentance,
<term>Repentance: In Böhme's writing, the German word "Busse" (repentance) refers to a total internal "turning around" or transformation of the soul, rather than just feeling sorry for sins.</term>
->The First Little Book;<-

How a person must awaken themselves in their will and mind; and what their contemplation and serious intention should be when they wish to perform powerful repentance; and with what kind of spirit they should approach GOD when they wish to ask for and receive the forgiveness of sins.

<image-desc>A horizontal divider made of decorative cross-shaped fleurons.</image-desc>

I. OF TRUE REPENTANCE, Book 1.
<note>original Latin: *DE POENITENTIA VERA, Lib. 1.*</note>

I wish to warn you, however: if you are not in earnest, then leave the precious names of GOD (by which the highest Holiness is named, invoked, and powerfully desired) alone, lest they kindle the wrath of GOD in your soul. For one should not misuse the holy name of GOD. This little book belongs only to those who gladly wish to perform repentance and are in the desire for the beginning <note>Meaning: those who have the initial yearning to begin their spiritual journey.</note>; they will experience in both ways <note>Both through inner spiritual experience and outer understanding.</note> what kind of words are within it, and from what source they are born. Herewith commended to the eternal goodness and mercy of GOD!

<image-desc>A large, ornate drop cap "W" decorated with detailed floral patterns and leafy vines.</image-desc>

1. When a person wishes to proceed to repentance and turn to GOD with their prayer, they should, before all prayer, contemplate their <term>mind (Gemüht): The seat of the soul’s will, thoughts, and desires.</term>, how the same stands so completely and utterly turned away from GOD; how it has become unfaithful to GOD; how it is directed only toward the temporal, fragile, earthly life, and bears no right love toward GOD and one’s neighbor; and how it thus lusts and surges entirely against GOD’s commandment, seeking only itself in temporal, transitory fleshly lust.

3. Thirdly, they should contemplate the three cruel chains to which our soul is firmly bound during the time of this earthly life. The first is **GOD’s strict wrath**, the abyss and dark world, which is the <term>Center (Centrum): The foundational core or inner essence of a being's life.</term> and natural life of the soul. The second chain is the **devil’s desire** toward the soul, with which he constantly <note>original: *sichtet*; a reference to "sifting like wheat" from Luke 22:31.</note> sifts and tempts the soul, seeking without ceasing to plunge it from GOD’s truth into vanity—such as pride, greed, envy, and wrath—and constantly inflating and igniting those same evil qualities within the soul through his desire. By this, the soul’s will turns away from GOD and enters into a <term>Self-hood (Selbheit): The state of being centered on one’s own ego and desires rather than on God’s will.</term>. The third and most harmful chain to which the poor soul stands bound is the **corrupt and entirely vain, earthly, mortal flesh and blood**, full of evil desire and inclination. Here, they should contemplate how they are, with body and soul, held fast as a prisoner in the mire of sin, in GOD’s wrath, and in the maw of the abyss of hell—

# I. ON TRUE REPENTANCE, Book 1. <note>original: *DE POENITENTIA VERA, Lib. 1.*</note>

...for the sake of the pleasure of the eyes <note>original: *Augen-Lust*. In Böhme's context, this refers to the seductive, superficial beauty of the material world that distracts the soul from spiritual reality.</note> man gambles away such great bliss / and enters into such great eternal unrest for its sake.

<margin>In such contemplation he will feel it in his heart and mind.</margin>
9. In such contemplation, man will feel [himself] in his heart and mind <note>original: *Gemühte*. A complex term in German mysticism referring to the deep-seated disposition of the soul, encompassing the will, emotions, and thoughts.</note> / especially if he constantly sets his end before his eyes / so that he will receive a heartfelt longing and desire for **GOD'S** mercy; and he will begin to repent of his committed sins / that he has spent his days so poorly / and neither perceived nor considered how he stands here in this world like a field in growth / either as a fruit in **GOD'S** love or wrath; and he will only then realize / that he has yet worked nothing in **Christ's** vineyard / and that he is a dry branch on the vine of **Christ**: Then in many a person / whom the Spirit of **Christ** touches in such contemplation / a great misery and heartache / a lamenting within himself / will arise in abundance / over the days of his wickedness / which he has thus postponed and spent in vanity / without any effect in **Christ's** vineyard.

<margin>He who enters into repentance is very easily helped;</margin>

# I. Of True Repentance / 1st Book.
### B 5
<meta>catchword: instruction</meta>

Step before God in such a way that there is a firm resolution within, that the person will no longer walk in the old footsteps of vanity—and even if the whole world should consider him a fool for it, and even if he should lose honor and property, and even temporal life, he would still remain steadfast in this resolution.

<margin>Make a vow to the noble Sophia.</margin>
29. Such a vow he must make to the noble Sophia <note>Divine Wisdom, personified as the heavenly bride of the soul.</note> in his resolution and mind, if he wishes to obtain her marriage and love. For ***Christ*** also spoke thus: Whoever does not leave wife, children, brothers, sisters, money, property, and everything he has, yes, even his earthly life, and follow me, is not worthy of me. ***Christ*** understands this regarding the soul’s disposition; that if there were anything which would hold the mind back in this matter, however beautiful and glorious it might seem in this world, the mind should disregard it and would rather go without it than lose the love of the noble **Virgin Sophia** in the growth of the **Flower of Christ**, in his tender humanity within us, according to **heavenly corporeality** <note>original: *Himlischer Leiblichkeit*. The spiritual substance or "resurrection body" that Böhme believes grows within the believer.</note>. For that is the **Flower of Sharon**, the **Rose of the Valley** <note>Biblical imagery from the Song of Solomon, interpreted here as the blossoming of Christ within the soul.</note>, of which Solomon plays <note>original: *spielet*. Refers to the poetic and allegorical nature of the Song of Solomon.</note>, and calls his dear beloved his chaste virgin, whom he loved so much, just as all the saints before and after him have ever loved her; whoever has obtained her has called her his **Pearl** <note>Refers to the "Pearl of Great Price" (Matthew 13:45-46), a common mystical metaphor for the discovery of the divine light within.</note>. How one should pray for this follows hereafter in a short instruction—

<summary>Böhme explains that a total renunciation of worldly status and family ties is necessary to enter a spiritual "marriage" with Divine Wisdom (Sophia). > O life of my flesh and of the souls / in Christ / my brother! to you I cry / in my soul’s hunger / and pray to you with all my strength / although it is weak / yet give me / what you have gifted and promised me in my Savior **Jesus Christ** / namely his flesh for food / and his blood for drink / for the refreshment of my poor, hungry soul / so that it might become strong in your **Word** / which became human <note>original: "Mensch ward" — referring to the Incarnation.</note> / and revive itself / whereby it might become truly longing and hungry for you.

# I. OF TRUE REPENTANCE, Book 1. <note>original: "DE POENITENTIA VERA, Lib. 1."</note>

## Warning to the Reader.

31. Out of good intentions, I will not hide from you, dear reader, what has been earnestly shown to me: If you are still comfortable in the vanity of the flesh, and are not in earnest purpose on the way to the new rebirth, with the will to become another person, then leave the transcribed words in this prayer unspoken; otherwise, they will become a judgment of **God** within you. You shall not misuse the holy names; be faithfully warned: they belong to the thirsty soul. If the soul is truly in earnest, she will experience what they are.

### Instruction on how the soul should meet her Beloved <term>Buhlen: A term used in 17th-century mystical literature to describe the "Beloved" or "Suitor," referring here to the spiritual marriage between the soul and Christ or Divine Wisdom.</term>, when he knocks in the Center <term>Centro: In Böhme's philosophy, the "Center" is the inner spiritual core of a person where the divine light can be birthed.</term>, in the locked chamber of the soul.

<margin>
Seriousness 
must 
be;
</margin>
32. Dear soul, there must be seriousness, without ceasing. You may indeed obtain the love of a kiss from the noble *Sophia* <note>Sophia is the Greek word for "Wisdom." In Böhme’s theology, she represents the "Eternal Virgin" or the mirror of God’s wisdom that the soul lost at the Fall and seeks to reunite with.</note>, in the holy name of **JESUS**, for she stands even without that before the soul’s door and knocks, and warns the sinner of the godless way. If the soul now desires her love, she is willing to grant it, and kisses the soul with the rays of her sweet love, from which the heart receives joy; but she does not immediately lie down in the marriage-bed with the soul; that is, she does not immediately awaken the faded heavenly—

# I. OF TRUE REPENTANCE, Book 1.

...heavenly image, which faded in Paradise, into itself. There is danger for man; for since **Adam** and **Lucifer** fell, so it might well happen again, as long as man stands so strongly bound in vanity.

<margin>Must be tested</margin>
33. It must be a faithful bond of your promise if she <note>Refers to Sophia, or Divine Wisdom, mentioned on the previous page.</note> is to crown you; you must first be tested. She withdraws her rays of love from you again and sees whether you will remain faithful; she may even let you stand and answer you nothing, not even with a glance of her love. For if she is to crown you, you must first be judged, so that you taste the sour beer <note>A 17th-century idiom equivalent to "reaping what one has sown," referring here to the bitter consequences of one's own sins.</note> which you have poured for yourself in your abominations. You must first go before the gates of hell and prove your victory—for the sake of and within her love, by the power with which she gazed upon you—against the gaze of the devil.

<margin>like *Christ*.</margin>
34. *Christ* was tempted in the wilderness; if you wish to put Him on <note>A reference to the biblical concept of "putting on the new man" or "putting on Christ" (Galatians 3:27).</note>, then you must go through his entire *process* <term>Process: In Böhme’s theology, the "process" is the necessary sequence of spiritual transformation that mirrors Christ's life, death, and resurrection.</term>, from his incarnation even unto his ascension. Although you cannot nor are required to do what He did, you must nonetheless enter completely into his *process* and continually die to the soul's vanity within his *process*. For the Virgin *Sophia* betroths herself to the soul in no other way than in this quality, which blossoms forth in the soul through *Christ's* death as a new ge- <note>The text cuts off here; likely "new growth" (Gewächs) or "new birth" (Geburt).</note>

...growth <note>original: "Gewächse"; referring to the spiritual "plant" of the new man or the fruit of faith.</note> that stands in heaven. The earthly body does not grasp her <note>Referring to Sophia, or Divine Wisdom.</note> during this time, for it must first die away from vanity; but the heavenly image—which faded in **Adam**—as the true **seed of the woman** <note>A reference to the messianic promise in Genesis 3:15, which Böhme interprets as the spark of divinity restored in the soul.</note> in which **God** became man and into which He led His living seed of heavenly essence, that image grasps the precious pearl, in the same way as occurred in **Mary** at the fulfillment of this covenant.

<margin>The promise must be kept.</margin>
35. Therefore, take heed of what you do; if you make a promise, keep it. She <note>Divine Wisdom.</note> would rather crown you than you yourself desire. But you must see to it: when the tempter approaches you with the world’s pleasure, beauty, and glory, the mind <term>Gemühte: A central term in Böhme’s writing, referring to the "mind-soul" or the seat of the will and emotions.</term> must reject it and say: "I am to be a servant in the vineyard of *Christ*, and not the master; of everything I have, I am only a servant of **God**, and I shall do with it as His Word teaches me; my heart shall be simple <note>original: "albern"; in this period, it meant simple-hearted, sincere, or innocent rather than foolish.</note> in the dust and always humble."

<margin>Humility must be at the forefront.</margin>
36. Whatever your station in life may be, humility must stand at the forefront, otherwise you will not achieve her marriage <note>The mystical union between the soul and Divine Wisdom.</note>; although true humility is only born within that marriage. But the free will of the soul must stand like a knight. For if the devil cannot prevail through the vanity of the soul—if she will not take his bait—then he comes with unworthiness and the register of sins; then the struggle truly begins.

<margin>The Devil's opposition.</margin>
37. Here, the merit of **Christ** must be placed at the forefront; otherwise, the creature cannot triumph over the **Devil**. For in this stage, things go terribly for many, so much so that even outward reason thinks this person is senseless <note>original: "Sinn-los"; meaning irrational or out of one's mind.</note> and possessed by the **Devil**. So cruelly does the **Devil** resist in many, especially if he has held a great **robber-castle** <note>original: "Raub-Schloß"; a metaphor for a spiritual stronghold or a deeply ingrained sinful habit where the soul was held captive.</note> within them, when he is meant to yield and leave his stronghold. Here it is a matter of fighting, where **Heaven** and **Hell** contend with one another.

<margin>He who triumphs is crowned.</margin>
38. If the **soul** now remains steadfast here, and triumphs over the **Devil** in all his attacks, and regards all temporal things as nothing for the sake of the love of her noble **Sophia** <note>Divine Wisdom, often personified by Böhme as the soul's heavenly bride.</note>, then the precious **knight’s wreath** <note>original: "Ritter-Cränzlein"; a symbol of spiritual victory and divine favor awarded to the "soldier of Christ."</note> is placed upon her as a sign of victory. Here the **Virgin**—who reveals herself out of the precious name of **JESUS**, with **Christ** the **Crusher of the Serpent** <note>original: "Schlangen-Treter"; a reference to the promise in Genesis 3:15 that the offspring of the woman would crush the head of the serpent.</note> as the **Anointed of GOD**—steps toward the soul and kisses her with her sweetest love quite inwardly in her very essence, and imprints her love as a victory sign into the soul's desire. And here **Adam**, according to his heavenly part, rises from death in **Christ**. I cannot write of this; there is no pen in this world capable of it, for it is the **Marriage of the Lamb** where the noble **pearl** is sown—indeed with great triumph, though at first it is as small as a **mustard seed**, as **Christ** says.

In this way, **Christ** covers his bride under the **Cross** so that she is not recognized in this world; the **Devil** also does this, so that these children remain hidden from the world, lest many such branches grow in his supposed garden. I set this down for the reader of a Christian mind for their instruction, should they also encounter such things, to know what they must do.

40. O most profound love of God in Christ Jesus! Do not forsake me in this distress; for I am indeed guilty of the sins which now rise up in my conscience; if You forsake me, I must sink. You have inscribed me in Your hands—in Your hands pierced through with sharp nails—and in Your hollowed side, from which blood and water flowed <note>A reference to John 19:34; in Böhme’s theology, the "hollowed side" of Christ is a mystical refuge for the soul.</note>, You have engraved me. I sink into You in the anguish of my conscience; do with me as You will. ### To this, the Virgin Sophia speaks to the Soul:

47. My noble bridegroom, my strength and power, be welcomed many times over; how have you forgotten me for so long, that I had to stand knocking at your door in great mourning? This term suggests an illicit paramour, signifying the soul's spiritual adultery with worldly vanity.</note> who has so defiled you and built his robber-castle of vanity <note>original: "Raub-Schloß der Eitelkeit." A vivid image of the ego-driven mind that "robs" God of his glory to serve its own pride.</note> within you, and turned you completely away from my love and faithfulness into his hypocritical, false kingdom; therein you have worked much sin and wickedness, and broken your will away from my love, and have committed adultery against me, and [taken] a strange...

[You have] pursued illicit love <note>original: "Buhlschaft." All the holy angels now rejoice with us, seeing us in this marriage again. Now, my dear beloved, <note>original: "Buhle." While often meaning a paramour, here Sophia uses the term affectionately to signify their restored spiritual intimacy.</note> remain in my faithfulness and turn your face from me no more; work your wonders in my love, for which God has awakened you.

## The noble Sophia answers the soul most comfortingly and says:

51. My noble **bridegroom**, be comforted! I have betrothed myself to you in my highest love and bound myself to you in my faithfulness. I will be with you and in you every day until the end of the world. I will come to you and make my dwelling in your **inner choir** <term>inner choir: a mystical term for the deepest part of the human spirit, envisioned as a sanctuary or the "holy of holies" where the soul meets God.</term> within you. You shall drink from my little fountain, for I am now yours and you are mine; the enemy shall not part us. Work within your **fiery property** <note>original: "feurischer Eigenschaft." In Böhme's thought, the soul's nature is essentially "fire"—a driving, hungry energy that must be tempered by the "light" of divine love to become a holy flame rather than a consuming heat.</note>, and I will instill my rays of love into your acting. We will cultivate the vineyard of **Jesus Christ**. You provide the **essence** of **fire**, and I will provide the **essence** of **light** and **growth** <note>original: "Gedeyen." This implies thriving, prosperity, or spiritual fruitfulness.</note>. Be you **fire**, and I will be **water** <note>Water here represents the "meekness" or the cooling, life-giving substance of the divine light that balances the soul's fire.</note>. We will perform that in this world for which we are ordained by **God**, and we will serve **Him** in His **temple**, which we ourselves are. **Amen.**

### A Little Evening Prayer.

54. 64 ON REPENTANCE <note>original: "DE POENITENTIA"</note>, Book 2.

# On True Repentance

### Or: The Second Little Book;

## A brief indication of the key to the understanding of Divine Mystery / how man may attain Divine Contemplation within himself.

---

Whosoever wishes to attain to Divine Contemplation <term>contemplation: "Beschaulichkeit," a state of quiet, prayerful observation of the divine presence within the soul.</term> within himself / and to speak with God in Christ / let him follow this process, and so he shall arrive at it.

<margin>Man should take up a serious intent,</margin>
1. He should gather all his senses and reason / together with all imagination / into a Single Mind / and conceive within himself such a strong **imagination** <term>imagination: in this context, it refers to a focused spiritual visualization or the "shaping" of the will toward a divine object.</term> as to contemplate himself, what he may be; seeing that the Scripture calls him God's image (Genesis 1:27), indeed, a temple of the **Holy Spirit** (1 Corinthians 6:19) who dwells within him; and calls him a member of Christ's body / and offers him Christ’s flesh and blood as food and drink.

<margin>examine himself,</margin>
2. Thus he should behold himself in his life / to see if he is worthy of this great grace / and capable of this high title of Christ / and begin to contemplate <note>The word "betrachten" (to contemplate) begins on this page and concludes the thought from the previous page.</note> his whole life—

<summary>This page introduces the second book on True Repentance, focusing on the internal process of "Divine Contemplation." It instructs the reader to look inward and recognize their status as an image of God and a temple of the Holy Spirit.</summary>

<keywords>True Repentance, Divine Contemplation, Image of God, Temple of the Holy Spirit, Imagination, Self-Examination</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the self-examination process introduced at the end of page 64. It explores the internal "will" of the individual as a sign of God's grace and the "in-spoken" word.</meta>

I. ON TRUE REPENTANCE / Book 2.

—what he has done / and how he has spent his entire time? Whether he also finds himself in Christ? Whether he also stands within the Divine Will / or toward what he is inclined? Whether he also finds any will within himself / that heartily longs for GOD / and would gladly be saved?

<margin>Beholding the will.</margin>
3. And if he now finds a deep-hidden will within himself / that would gladly turn toward God's grace / if only it could; let him know / that this very will is the **incorporated** <term>incorporated: "eingeleibte," referring to the divine Word becoming a structural part of the human essence.</term> / and—after the commission of sin—the **in-spoken** <term>in-spoken: "eingesprochene," the promise of grace or "Gospel" spoken by God into the soul in Paradise after the Fall.</term> Word of GOD; that then the GOD JEHOVAH, as the Father / draws him toward Christ: For in our own **selfhood** <term>selfhood: "Eigenheit," the state of fallen man acting out of his own ego rather than in harmony with God.</term> we no longer have any will toward obedience.

4. But this same drawing of the Father / as the incorporated / in-spoken grace / draws all people / even the most godless (provided he be not an absolute thistle <note>In Boehme's imagery, a "thistle" refers to a person who has become so hardened and prickly in their wickedness that they refuse to yield to God's influence.</note> / and is willing to stand still for a moment for that drawing) away from his false activity.

5. Thus no man has cause to doubt God's grace / so long as he finds within himself a desire / to eventually be converted.

6. Let the same man not delay it for another moment / as it is written: Today / if you hear the Lord's voice / then harden not your ears and hearts. <note>A reference to Psalm 95:7-8 and Hebrews 3:7-8.</note>

# I. ON TRUE REPENTANCE, Book 2. <note>original: "DE POENITENTIA VERA, Lib. 2."</note>

<margin>The desire for conversion is **God’s** voice.</margin>
...conversion is **God’s** voice within man, which the devil covers and delays with his introduced images <term>images: "Bilden," referring to the mental constructs, worldly distractions, or internal idols that preoccupy the mind and obscure divine presence.</term>, so that it is postponed from one day and year to the next, until finally the soul becomes a thistle <note>As established on the previous page, the "thistle" represents a heart so hardened by sin that it is no longer receptive to the "dew" of God's grace.</note> and can no longer reach grace.

<margin>Should examine his life in **God’s** commandments.</margin>
8. ### II. SIGHS OF THE WAYFARERS <note>original Latin: *SUSPIRIA VIATORUM*. In seventeenth-century devotional literature, the "wayfarer" is the soul traveling through the earthly wilderness toward a heavenly home.</note>
->Or:<-

# On Holy Prayer/
->Directed<-
## Toward Every Day of the Week.

How a person should constantly remember their **calling** <term>calling: the German *Amt* refers to one's specific duty, professional role, or office as ordained by God</term> / station / and conduct / in which God has placed them : and how they should entrust their beginning / middle / and end in all their deeds to God / and always perform all their works with God ; just as the branch of a tree produces its twigs with the power of the root / and bears its fruit upon them : and how they should draw strength from God’s small fountain for all their workings at every beginning / and thank their Creator for every good deed.

### Alongside Heartfelt Contemplation of the Suffering / Death and Resurrection of JEsus Christ.

How a person should always lead the hunger and desire of their soul through the death of Christ into His resurrection in God / and press toward the new **rebirth**; so that they may pray in spirit and in truth / and that the Spirit of Christ may pray in and with them / and intercede for them before God.

<meta>catchword: Vor= (likely for *Vorrede* or Preface)</meta>

<summary>This page introduces a new series of daily prayers, establishing the theological principle that a believer's daily work and spiritual life must be rooted in God, much like a branch is rooted in a tree. It emphasizes that true prayer is performed through the Spirit of Christ within the individual.</summary>

<keywords>prayer, calling, station in life, Christ's passion, rebirth, spiritual root, daily devotion, intercession</keywords>

II. On Holy Prayer. Preface.

# The Author’s Preface to the God-loving Reader.
## On the True Foundation of the Right Art of Prayer / What Prayer Is / and Why God Commands Us to Pray.

<margin>Prayer is not merely an ordinary speaking of words.</margin>
Dear Christian reader! Praying rightly is not merely a habit / such that one need only speak the words of prayer: No; such speaking of words / without heartfelt devotion and divine desire / is only an external thing / an external formation of words.

<margin>The mouth only creates a form of the will without power</margin>
2. The mouth forms its words of prayer with the external power of the stars <term>stars: in seventeenth-century mysticism, the *Gestirne* or stars refer to the astrological and natural forces that govern physical existence</term> and the elements / and creates only a form of the will / in which there is no actual power. For nothing pleases God / except what He Himself works and does through a thing. <note>The author suggests that true prayer is a divine act happening *through* the person, rather than a human performance.</note>

<margin>about which God complains through the Prophet.</margin>
3. For God complains through the Prophet about such external mouth-prayer without power / when He says: "They draw near to me with their lips / but their heart is far from me," Isaiah 29:13. Likewise / Christ says: "Not all / who say: Lord, Lord! shall enter into the kingdom of heaven / but those who do the will—"

<meta>catchword: will—</meta>

<summary>This preface introduces the "art of prayer," emphasizing that true prayer must come from a heart filled with divine desire rather than being a mechanical recitation of words. The author argues that empty prayers lack spiritual power because they are driven by physical nature rather than God's spirit.</summary>
<keywords>prayer, devotion, hypocrisy, stars and elements, Isaiah 29:13, will of God, heart vs. lips</keywords>

## II. SIGHS OF THE PILGRIMS. Preface.
<note>original: "SUSPIRIA VIATORUM. Præfat." The Latin title "Suspiria Viatorum" translates to "Sighs of the Travelers" or "Sighs of the Pilgrims," referring to the soul's journey through this earthly life toward heaven.</note>

but for **GOD** and our neighbor; and that we altogether in **Christ** our **Lord** are only One / who is He himself in us all / And that we therefore should have a common love among one another / and desire to love each other from the heart / as **GOD** has loved us in **Christ** our Savior. And that we wish right gladly to share the gifts / which **GOD** gives us through our prayer / be they heavenly or earthly / with our fellow members / and conduct ourselves like the tree among its branches / or as the earth does with its fruits / which willingly yields itself to all its fruits / and loves and bears them all.

<margin>Not by our own strength.</margin>
8. Eighthly, we should consider / that we cannot pray rightly before **GOD** by our own strength / as **Christ** says: Without me you can do nothing / John 15:5. And St. Paul also says: We do not know what we should pray / as is fitting before **GOD** / but the Holy Spirit himself intercedes for us mightily with wordless sighs <note>The "sighs" or "groanings" mentioned here link directly to the book's title, "Suspiria."</note> before **GOD** / as it pleases him / Romans 8:26.

<margin>In the Name of **Jesus**.</margin>
9. Therefore, if we wish to pray to **GOD** our heavenly Father / then we should call upon him in the name of his dear Son **Jesus Christ** for the illumination of his Holy Spirit / that He would [forgive] us our sins for the sake of His bitter suffering

## II. On Holy Prayer. Preface. 79

and for the sake of [His] death forgive [us] / and give us what is good and leads to salvation. We should place everything that is earthly into His knowledge and will / and not step before **GOD** with mere breath and words / if we want to pray rightly and be heard; but with truly earnest repentance and a turning away from our false way of life.

<margin>Should flee sin, and surrender ourselves entirely to **GOD**.</margin>
10. We should depart from all falsehood / pride / greed / envy / anger / and resentment / and surrender our entire heart and soul to **GOD** the Holy Spirit / so that He may be the one who works repentance and strength in our prayer; that He may take our will and desire into Himself / and lead them into **GOD** / so that we might die to our false vanity and desire / which we have inherited <note>Refers to the concept of original sin or "inherited" fallen nature.</note> / in the death of **Christ** / and be raised and born within us with a new will / mind / and obedience toward **GOD** in the spirit of **Christ**; and from now on walk before **GOD** in such power / in righteousness and purity / with our will and new birth / as His children / whom He has dearly purchased through the blood and death of His dear Son / and has reborn in His Spirit.

### II. On Holy Prayer. Preface.

I cannot hide from my friends and fellow brothers in Christian love what I have drawn from my little treasure chest <note>original: "Schatzkästlein." A common 17th-century term for a collection of spiritual gems or devotional writings meant for private use.</note>, as a Christian reminder of how a person should be prepared for prayer. And although I know that they themselves stand beside me in such spiritual work and are partakers and capable of the gift of the Holy Spirit: yet I still wished to refresh myself somewhat with them in this and in the following prayers, and to indicate and share the grace of God with them from my own strength and gift; (just as one light ignites another, so too does one gift of God ignite another). This is so that we may refresh ourselves in the one Love—which is Christ in us all—and that I too may enjoy your divine gift and knowledge, so that together we may grow in the praise of God, increase, and bear much fruit.

26. And I have written the following prayers for every day (whereby a person may set aside their own special hours for them as opportunity allows) only as an encouragement and an awakening <note>original: "Erweckung." This refers to the stirring up of innate spiritual potential rather than teaching new information.</note> of the good gifts which are already within you all. I do not intend to start once again from the ground and beginning of Christian doctrine; rather, these are only for the purpose of practice and awakening. And I commend you all into the active love of Jesus Christ, and myself into your brotherly and Christian favor.
