# II. On Holy Prayer. Instruction.

<margin>A sigh is effective with GOD.</margin>
A single sigh <note>In 17th-century mystical thought, a "sigh" (*Seufzer*) is a technical term for an inward, wordless groan of the soul that reaches God more directly than long, formal prayers.</note> is effective with GOD, provided that the will stands purely before GOD and has cast off the earthly garment—namely, false lust. This is for the reader as a reminder and for his own encouragement.

37. For it is not necessary that only such a form of confession be used; rather, the Holy Spirit will surely create a form within the heart himself, whenever the will turns toward GOD with true earnestness.

38. Only as a guide for those who do not yet know how a true petitioner should be prepared <note>original: "geschickt," meaning properly disposed or spiritually ready.</note>, I will set down this confession to guide their soul. And I will entrust the work of confession, along with the prayer itself, to the Holy Spirit in every soul that is truly in earnest; He will surely provide the confession and prayers for them. If one only approaches the gateway with true earnestness—where GOD the Lord actually speaks within the human being—then they will perceive it.

## II. Of Holy Prayer. On Monday.

> O **Lord Jesus Christ** / clothe me in the garment of your innocence / in which you / while wearing our garment / took upon yourself the scorn of all people. ## II. Of Holy Prayer. On Tuesday. 129

[...be] separated from your face / and eternally not see your glory / nor come to your rest.

O great / holy **GOtt** <note>The capitalization of "GOtt" (God) and "HErr" (Lord) reflects the 17th-century printing convention of emphasizing divine names.</note> / who are a consuming fire / what should I poor / miserable man / who am full of disobedience / own lust and will / and have no right love nor inclination toward you / say before you? What should I answer you / if you place me before your judgment / and test my heart and soul?

O dear **GOtt** / I can do nothing! I stick in the mud of vanity even into the inner depths of the soul; Your anger is kindled in me: Within me live all evil beasts <note>In the mystical tradition, "beasts" or "animals" often represent the various worldly passions and vices (like pride, greed, or lust) that inhabit the fallen human soul.</note> with their lust.

Break, then, the Devil’s Sabbath and false influence <term>Wirkung: in this context, it refers to the internal "operation" or "working" of spiritual forces</term> within me, and give me an obedient heart that always hungers for your Sabbath. O you eternal love of **Jesus Christ**! How glorious is your Sabbath in the soul when it turns toward you, so that you permeate it with your sweet love, in which Paradise is opened to it once more. Let my soul remain in your Sabbath forever; rebuild within me the new **Jerusalem**, as the City of **God**, in which your Sabbath is sanctified. Into your Sabbath I surrender myself completely: deliver me only from evil, Amen.

### III. ON TRUE RESIGNATION <note>original: "Gelassenheit." A central concept in German mysticism referring to the process of letting go of the self-will to allow God to act through the soul.</note>. Ch. 1.

### III. ON EQUANIMITY <note>original: "DE ÆQUANIMITATE." In Böhme's context, this refers to "Gelassenheit" or spiritual resignation/composure.</note>. Ch. 1.

[Reason] thinks she is the beautiful child in the house,
but the devil is at home within her.

<margin>This is how it goes for all who deviate from resignation.</margin>
18. And so it goes for all those who, having once been enlightened by **God**, depart again from true *resignation* <term>resignation: "Gelassenheit," the mystical practice of yielding the individual will to God</term> and wean themselves from the true mother’s milk—that is, from right humility.

## The Process of a True Christian: How He Ought to Walk.

19. Reason wants to stop me and say: it is indeed right and good that a person attains both the light of **God** and the light of external nature and reason, so that he may govern his life wisely, according to the Scriptures.

<margin>The light of reason is indeed good,</margin>
20. Yes, it is right, and nothing more useful can happen to a person, nor can anything better befall him; it is a treasure above all treasures of this world for whoever can reach and obtain the light of **God** and of time <note>The "light of time" refers to understanding the natural, physical world</note>, for it is an eye of time and eternity.

<margin>and gives knowledge of selfhood.</margin>
21. But hear how you should use it: The light of **God** opens itself first in the soul; it shines forth like a light from a candle and immediately kindles the external light of reason. This does not mean that it surrenders itself entirely to the governance of reason, as the external man; no, the external man beholds himself in the [piercing...] <note>The text cuts off here at the catchword "drin-," likely meaning "dringenden" (piercing/urgent) light.</note>

### III. On True Equanimity <note>original: "Gelassenheit." This refers to the spiritual state of yielding or resignation to God's will.</note>. Ch. 1.

...penetrating gleam, like an image before a mirror; he immediately learns to know himself within his selfhood <term>selfhood: "Selbheit," the individual’s ego or sense of being a separate, independent entity</term>, which in itself is good and useful.

<margin>Reason, however, should not gaze upon itself therein.</margin>
22. When this happens, reason—as creaturely selfhood—can do nothing better than to refrain from gazing at itself within the selfhood of the creature. It must certainly not enter into the center <term>center: "Centrum," the inner core of the soul where the divine or dark fire resides</term> with the will of desire and seek itself. Otherwise, it breaks itself off from **God’s** Essence (which arises within the Light of **God**, and upon which the soul should feed and refresh itself); instead, it eats from the external light and essence, through which it draws the poison back into itself.

<margin>The will of the creature should consider itself unworthy of this grace.</margin>
23. The will of the creature should, with all its reason and desire, sink itself completely away, like an unworthy child who is not at all worthy of this high grace. It should not attribute any knowledge or understanding to itself, nor ask for or desire any understanding from **God** within its creaturely selfhood. Instead, it should simply and humbly sink into the love and grace of **God** in **Christ JESUS**. It should desire to be as if dead to its own reason and selfhood within the life of **God**, surrendering itself entirely to **God’s** life in love, so that He may do with it as His instrument, however and whatever He wills.

### III. ON EQUANIMITY <note>original: "DE ÆQUANIMITATE." This relates to the German concept of 'Gelassenheit,' or the state of being surrendered and resigned to God's will.</note>. Ch. 1.

When the outward reason triumphs in the light and says, "I have the true child," then the will of desire should bend it to the earth and lead it into the highest humility and simple lack of understanding <note>original: "albern Unverstand." Everything that is in and on you must consider itself naught, as merely an instrument of God. 25. ### III. ON RESIGNATION. <note>original: "DE AEQUANIMITATE." From the Latin meaning composure, equanimity, or the state of being surrendered.</note> Ch. 1. 158

Then the creature is no longer its own possession, but rather the instrument of **GOD**.

<margin>The soul’s will must constantly sink into humility before God.</margin>
30. <margin>The light of reason should see through God’s light.</margin>
35. I do not say that man should not research, experience, and learn anything in the natural arts; no, for that is useful to him. However, one's own reason should not be the beginning. Man should not govern his life solely through the external **light of reason**; that light is certainly good, but he should sink himself with it into the deepest humility before **GOD**, and place the Spirit and will of **GOD** at the forefront of all his searching, so that the **light of reason** sees through **GOD’s** light. And though reason may come to know much, it should not claim this knowledge as its own property, but rather give the glory to **GOD**, to whom alone knowledge and wisdom belong.

He is pleased only by those who fear and bow before Him.

<margin>Self-will governs itself; the surrendered will trusts GOD.</margin>
37. For **GOD** did not create us for self-interest <note>original: "Eigenheit." The idea that the human soul is a tool or musical instrument played by the Holy Spirit.</term> of His wonders, through which He wishes to reveal His wonders Himself. The surrendered will trusts **GOD** and hopes for all good from Him; but the self-will governs itself, for it has broken itself off from **GOD**.

<margin>Whatever the self-will does is sin.</margin>
38. Everything that the self-will does is sin and against **GOD**. For it has gone out of the order in which **GOD** created it, into a state of disobedience, wanting to be its own master.

<margin>When it dies to selfhood, it is free from sin.</margin>
39. When the self-will dies to selfhood <note>original: "Selbheit." The quality of being focused on the "I" or the ego.</note>, it is free from sin. For it desires nothing but what **GOD** desires from His creature; it desires to do only that for which **GOD** created it, and what **GOD** wishes to do through it. And though it is and must be the one doing the acting, it is nonetheless only the instrument of the action, with which **GOD** does what He wills.

40. For that is exactly the right faith...

# Of the dawning Lily-Time.

To want nothing else for Christ than what Christ alone wants in him; even if he must suffer, so that his good fruits—which sprout and grow from the <term>inner man<note>The "inner man" (inwendiger Mensch) refers to the spiritual, eternal part of a person that is reborn through faith, as opposed to the "outer man" of the physical body.</note></term>—are often trampled and spoiled by his wicked flesh and blood, as well as by the devil's wind and the malice of the world, yet the tree of the new growth remains standing in the life of Christ, and blossoms out through the outer, mortal man without any stopping, just as eternity blossoms through time and gives life and power to time. And as the day blossoms through the night and transforms the night into day, and yet the night remains in itself but is not recognized within the day; so too the divine day blossoms out through our eternal night within us, and transforms the night—as God's wrath, hell, death, anguish, and eternal destruction—into the divine day of the kingdom of joy, even though the dark night, with the <term>serpent's essence<note>original: "Schlangen Ens." The word "Ens" is Latin for "being" or "essence." This refers to the poisonous nature of original sin inherited from the fall of man.</note></term> and venom in flesh and blood, rages and fights against it.

<margin>Opinions</margin>
§ 1. Therefore, beloved sir and Christian brother, we must strive more for the growth of the noble <term>Pearl-Tree<note>A common mystical metaphor for the development of the soul or the presence of Christ within the believer.</note></term>, and how we may attain it, than to run after useless chatter and trifles, where one brother [attacks] the other for the sake of an opinion that he has made... <note>The sentence is interrupted by the page break.</note>

# The 6th Epistle.
## To N. N. of Lübeck.
-> From April 20, 1624. <-

<margin>
The
55th
Letter
in the
Works.

-> **Our salvation in the active love** <-
-> **of JESUS Christ in us!** <-

My very dear and Christian Lord, high peace alongside hearty, member-like <note>original: "gliedlicher." Böhme uses this term to refer to the "members" or parts of the mystical Body of Christ, suggesting a communal, organic connection between believers.</note>, and in desire co-operating <note>original: "mitwirkender." A reference to synergy between the human will and divine grace.</note> well-wishing of love, that the sun of the actual love of **JESUS** Christ may constantly rise and shine in the Lord's soul, spirit, and body.

2. I received your letter dated January 24th fourteen days after Easter, and rejoiced to see therein that the Lord is a hungry, zealous, and eager seeker and lover of the true ground <term>Ground: "Grund" or "Ungrund" in Böhme’s thought refers to the foundational depth of the Divine or the source of all being.</term>; which, as I understand, the Lord has diligently pursued and searched for.

3. But that my writings have also come into your hands, and that you [the same]...

<header>OF THE DAWNING LILY-TIME.</header>

[...pleased you] <note>The text continues from the previous page's unfinished sentence regarding the recipient's interest in Böhme's writings.</note>; this is certainly the cause, and a Divine order, which leads the lovers toward love, and often uses strange means through which He fulfills the loving desire, feeds it with His gift, and introduces their love to a goal <note>original: "Ens"</note> of true fire-burning.

<margin>The searching must begin in the soul.</margin>
4. And the Lord shall surely believe, so far as he will remain constant in love for the truth, that it will open itself to him in fire-flaming love and make itself rightly known: Only that it be started rightly: For not through our sharp reason and searching do we attain the true ground of Divine knowledge <term>Knowledge: "Erkentniß" in Böhme’s vocabulary implies an experiential, spiritual realization rather than just factual learning.</term>; the searching must begin from within in the hunger of the soul, for reason-searching goes only into its *starry influence* <note>original: "Astrum"</note> <term>Astrum: Refers to the "star" or astral spirit, the level of human intellect governed by the physical cosmos/zodiac, which Böhme distinguishes from the deeper soul-spirit.</term> in the outer world, from which reason originates: But the soul searches in its *starry influence*, as in the inner spiritual world, from which the visible world emerged or flowed out <note>original: "ausgefloffen" (out-flowed / emanated).</note>, wherein it stands with its ground.

<margin>The soul must entirely surrender its power and will to love.</margin>
5. <summary>Böhme explains that true spiritual understanding comes not through intellectual effort or "reason-searching," but through a soulful hunger and a total surrender of the will to Divine Love. He distinguishes between the "Astrum" of the external world (reason) and the internal spiritual world where the soul truly resides.</summary>

what the Spirit of **GOD** wishes to search out through her <note>The soul ("die Seele") is grammatically feminine in German; Böhme uses "she" to describe the soul's relationship to God and Wisdom.</note>. And when she has thus surrendered herself / and thereby seeks nothing but **GOD'S** glory and her salvation / together with the service and love of the neighbor / and then finds herself in such a desire / that she would like to have Divine and natural knowledge <term>Knowledge: "Erkentniß" refers to a spiritual realization or "gnosis" that is granted by God, rather than learned through books.</term> / then she should know / that she is drawn to it by **GOD**; then she may well search such a deep ground / as is indicated in my writings.

7. My dear Sir / it is quite a simple, child-like way to the highest wisdom / the world does not know it: You do not need to search for it in distant places: She <note>Referring to "Weisheit" (Wisdom), often personified by Böhme as the Virgin Sophia.</note> stands before your soul’s door and knocks: If it be / that she may find an empty, surrendered <term>Surrendered: "gelassene" refers to "Gelassenheit," a central concept in German mysticism meaning a state of letting go of self-will to be filled by God.</term> place in the soul / then she will surely open herself there / and rejoice therein more than the sun in the elements; If she (the soul) gives herself to her (Wisdom) as her own possession / then she penetrates the soul with her fire-flaming love / and unlocks all mystery to her.

<note>original: "Christo entsprossen ist..." This completes the final sentence of the previous page.</note> ...joy, which has sprouted in Christ; and I hope to also enjoy its good fruits.

3. Just as one branch on a tree enjoys the sap and power of the others, and all grow and bear fruit in one single power; so we also in the Tree of Christ are all only One, which is Christ in us all.

4. Since you then freely and publicly profess this Tree of Life with mouth and heart, and conversely contradict Satan's poison and error, I wish for nothing more now than that I might be able—in the power of this tree, which is Christ—to pour into you my power received from Him, so that we, as members, may grow in One power.

5. <margin>The world speaks of Christ out of habit.</margin>
6. <margin>Earnestness and steadfastness are necessary.</margin>
13. Only consider what you have promised to this chaste **Sophia**; to hold it fast until your end; it must be done in earnest / not looking back toward **Sodom** / like **Lot’s wife** / who became a pillar of salt; but rather to go out from **Sodom** with **Lot** according to **God’s** desire / to enter into the following of **Christ** / regarding the world's mockery and blasphemy as nothing / but loving the **Mark of Christ** <note>This likely refers to the "stigmata" or the inner spiritual seal of suffering and humility that marks a true follower of Christ.</note> more than all the world's friendship / honor and goods / so that you may wander with us on **Christ’s Pilgrim-Path**: But if this does not taste good to you / and you still desire the world's pleasure and honor / then you are not yet fit to come to the wedding / and to your bride, our dear **Sophia**.

<margin>Repentance is not to be delayed.</margin>
14. Therefore consider well / and look at your whole heart: if it be that you notice a longing hunger and pull toward it (as I indeed perceive) / then delay not an hour / go forth / enter with earnestness into **Repentance** / and yield your will entirely to enter therein / and never [more]...

<meta>catchword: mer= (likely continuing to "merket" [perceive] or "mehr" [more])</meta>

<summary>Boehme describes the spiritual "Marriage of the Lamb" that occurs when the soul is victorious, warning the reader to remain steadfast and not look back at worldly temptations like Lot's wife. He urges immediate and total repentance for those who feel the inner "hunger" for Divine Wisdom.</summary>

<keywords>Sophia, Marriage of the Lamb, Shepherd's Staff, Sodom, Lot's wife, Repentance, Pilgrim-Path, spiritual victory</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the 7th Epistle of Jacob Boehme. The previous page ended with an exhortation to enter into earnest repentance and yield the will entirely to God.</meta>

<page-num>489</page-num>
->### Concerning the dawning Lily-Time <note>original: "Lilien-Zeit." In Boehme’s prophetic vision, this refers to a future age of spiritual flowering, peace, and the reign of the Holy Spirit on earth.</note> ###<-

...never more to depart from it; and though you should for its sake leave behind body, life, honor, and goods; if you do this, then you are rightly prepared, and the true **Wall-breaker** <note>original: "Maurbrecher." A Böhmean term for Christ or the Divine Spirit that breaks through the hard "wall" of the selfish ego and the material world.</note> will approach your soul and do within you that which you cannot do without Him.

<margin>
Wrath covers body and soul.
</margin>
15. And though afterward pain and great adversity might cling to your flesh, your **Reason** calling you a fool, and though **God’s Wrath** might overspread and cover you in body and soul; it will all not harm you; you will sprout forth with a new **Disposition** <note>original: "Gemühte." Refers to the internal seat of the soul's desires, will, and thoughts.</note> from among such thorns, and walk in heaven with the Spirit; even though the earthly body must interact with **creatures** <note>In this context, "creatures" refers to the physical elements and the material world.</note>, it will be with you as it is with a coarse stone in which beautiful gold grows.

<margin>
God covers the noble Pearl.
</margin>
16. You should not take offense at my tribulation, mockery, and persecution, nor fear anything because of it, for it is **Christ’s Mark**; only look back into the **Scripture**, how it fared with **God’s children**, how they were always persecuted and killed by those very people who were supposed to teach **God’s** ways: For a noble **Pearl** <note>original: "Perlein." A reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" (Matthew 13:45-46), representing the inner divine light or spiritual truth.</note> is entrusted to me, which **God** so covers up that the unworthy do not see it, but are blind to it, and take offense at the simplicity of the person, so that in their **Reason-Wisdom** <note>original: "Vernunft-Weisheit." Human intellect and logic that operates without divine illumination.</note> it remains foolish to them, even though they see it /

<sig>Hh 5</sig>
<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>Boehme encourages the reader to persevere through worldly suffering and the mockery of "Reason," promising that the Divine Spirit (the Wall-breaker) will transform the soul like gold growing inside a common stone. He explains that spiritual truth remains hidden from the "unworthy" who rely solely on human intellect.</summary>

<header>IX. 7. THEOSOPHICAL EPISTLES</header>

and yet they do not understand, because they disdain the form of Christ: But a time is drawing very near when they shall have to give a serious account of it.

<margin>
Protect the
simple-
hearted.
</margin>
17. But that God has given you to recognize what it is, and from whence it comes, thank Him for that; it has happened to you out of grace, and because you have humbled yourselves before Him; and even greater grace may befall you if you remain standing in humility and earnest prayer: I will gladly share my love with you by letter, and through Christ-like activity <note>original: "Christ-gliedlichem Wirken." This implies acting as a functional "member" or limb of the Body of Christ.</note>; for it is a pure <note>original: "eitele." In Boehme’s time, this often meant "sheer," "complete," or "pure," rather than the modern sense of "vain" or "conceited."</note> joy in my heart—even though I must suffer bodily tribulation because of it—yet I rejoice that I see what God the Lord has done through me, a poor man, thus far.

<margin>
Satan
cannot
hinder
God’s
way.
</margin>
18. Satan cannot hinder God's ways: For although it indeed appears as if he were hindering them with such murderous outcries <note>original: "Mord-Geschrey." Likely referring to the loud and aggressive public condemnations Boehme faced from local clergy.</note>, it is nevertheless revealed more and more so that God’s children inquire after the **Ground** <note>original: "Grund." A central term in Boehme’s philosophy referring to the underlying essence of God and reality.</note>.

19. The godless crowd, however, will be hardened and hindered by this, but the others will be called through it; you shall see this before a year has passed; and even if they were to kill me, it would still have to proceed, for it is from the Lord.

20. I commend you to the gentle, active love of Jesus Christ, and myself to your brotherly love and favor! Dated as above <note>Latin: "Datum ut supra." This indicates the letter shares the same date as a previous document or the collection's heading.</note>.

-> E N D . <-

<summary>In this concluding section of the 7th Epistle, Boehme encourages his followers to remain humble and prayerful, asserting that despite Satan's "murderous outcries" and his own personal suffering, the divine truth will continue to spread because it is ordained by the Lord.</summary>
<keywords>humility, grace, persecution, Satan, the Ground, Body of Christ, divine providence, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

Preface. judges from the outside 10. Preface. its false speaking 438.

--- Page 567 ---
<meta>This page follows the alphabetical index of Jacob Böhme’s works and appears to be a concluding pastoral exhortation or a summary of the mystical path described in the preceding texts. It focuses on the shift from external ritual to an "inward faith" where the presence of Christ is experienced within the soul rather than as an external historical figure.</meta>

Therefore, all my brothers and sisters in Christ <note>original: "Christo" — the Latin dative form of Christus.</note>, because we should now, through our **inward faith**, have such boldness <note>original: "freidigkeit" — a term often used in older German bibles to denote the confidence or "freeness" with which a believer approaches God.</note> and entrance to the holy **cleansing blood** of the **crucified one**, which He has also prepared for us as the new and living way, and toward the **new creature** <note>original: "Creatur" — in this context, referring to the "new creation" or "new man" mentioned in Pauline theology, the spiritual rebirth of the individual.</note> in His own flesh; and as we now have a true **high priest** over the **house of God**, let us then go forth with a single and true heart, with our sorrowful, **inward faith of repentance** <note>original: "buß glauben" — a faith born from "Busse," meaning both repentance and the internal process of turning away from the self toward God.</note>, sprinkled with love in our hearts, free from evil thoughts, and washed clean in body. 

We should also hold right fast to the true **knowledge** and confession of God and the certain hope, so that we do not waver or retreat from it, but rather rely confidently on His Word of God. For He Himself has said: "I will not leave you, nor forsake you, as long as you keep faithfulness and faith." We Christians should also kindly entice and stir one another toward Christian love and good works, so that our **inward faith** becomes beautifully perfect, that we may endure. 

Blessed is he who recognizes this light, also in spirit and truth, with his **inward faith**. He also belongs to those who say: "We are not of those who retreat, but of those who hold fast in inward faith," specifically Jesus the crucified **in us**, and not **outside of us**. In us, He makes us righteous and blessed <note>original: "seelig" — meaning both saved and filled with divine bliss.</note>. May God, by His grace, grant this to me and to all my brothers and sisters to enjoy, here in **temporality** <note>original: "Zeitigkeit" — refers to our time-bound, earthly existence.</note>, and there in **eternity**.

->Whoever now truly believes<-
->becomes blessed. Amen.<-

<summary>This concluding exhortation encourages believers to seek a direct, internal relationship with the "crucified Christ within," emphasizing inward repentance and faith over external forms. It promises that through this internal presence, one finds both righteousness and eternal bliss.</summary>

<keywords>inward faith, cleansing blood, high priest, new creature, repentance, Christ within, temporality, eternity, salvation</keywords>
